### 2026-09-08 (round 31) — Gengar back to 32x32 (the REAL cause found), randomizer menu input lockout (BUILT green, untested, UNCOMMITTED)
- **THE GENGAR ROOT CAUSE WAS A THIRD FILE.** Round 30 fixed the pic table and still rendered garbage because **`src/data/graphics/pokemon.h`** includes the sheet as `INCGFX_COMP(..., "-mwidth 4 -mheight 4")`, and gbagfx groups tiles into blocks of exactly that size. With 64x64 frames declared everywhere else, the converted tile ORDER was still 32x32 blocks, so the sprite could never assemble.
- **AN OVERWORLD SPRITE HAS THREE DEFINITIONS THAT MUST ALL AGREE, and a mismatch renders garbage rather than failing to build:** (1) the PNG's frame size, (2) `-mwidth`/`-mheight` on its INCGFX in `src/data/graphics/pokemon.h`, (3) `overworld_ascending_frames(..., w, h)` in the pic table **and** the `SIZE_` macro in the species table. Changing any subset silently corrupts the sprite.
- **Reverted to 32x32 per Joe** (he asked for about half the attempted size; the OAM only offers 32 or 64 square, so 32). `git checkout` restored the art, pic table back to `4, 4`, species back to `SIZE_32x32`. **The DistortionWorld object still uses `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_SPECIES_SHINY(GENGAR_MEGA)`** — the white Mega form, now at normal follower size. Verified all three definitions agree.
- **If large is ever wanted again**, it is now a known three-line change: upscale the PNG 2x, set `-mwidth 8 -mheight 8`, set the pic table and species size to 8,8 / SIZE_64x64.
- **Randomizer menu no longer loses the decision to mashing.** The cursor already defaulted to `MENUITEM_MASTER` and A only acts on `MENUITEM_START`, so the cause was **A carrying over from name entry**, which opens straight into this screen. Added a **30-frame input lockout** (`tInputDelay`, data[5]) armed when the fade completes; `Task_ProcessInput` swallows everything until it expires. Also replaced the bare `0`s with `MENUITEM_MASTER` so the intended starting row is stated rather than implied. **This menu sets a permanent, unchangeable-later choice — it must never be dismissable by momentum.**

### 2026-09-08 (round 30) — Gengar sprite fix, Draco flees north, starter signature moves, wild-double bug (BUILT green, untested, UNCOMMITTED)
- **GENGAR SPRITE FIXED — my bug from round 29.** `sPicTable_GengarMega` still declared `overworld_ascending_frames(..., 4, 4)` = 32x32 while the species table said `SIZE_64x64`. Frames were 512 bytes apart while the OAM read 2048, so every frame dragged in the next three and the sprite rendered as garbage. Now `8, 8`, matching Steelix. **RULE: the pic table's tile dimensions and the OVERWORLD() size macro must always agree; changing one without the other silently renders garbage rather than failing to build.**
- **WILD DOUBLE BATTLE BUG FOUND AND FIXED** (likely Joe's "battles randomly abandon after one attack"). All three double-wild sites in `src/wild_encounter.c` **ignored TryGenerateWildMon's return value**. It returns FALSE without calling CreateWildMon when Keen Eye/Intimidate or repel turns the encounter away, so `gEnemyParty[0]` kept the FIRST mon and `gEnemyParty[1] = mon1` copied it — a double battle against the same Pokemon twice, sharing one personality value. Now falls back to the already-generated single battle. **Upstream expansion bug, only reachable because round 29 turned doubles on.**
- **Wild double odds 10% -> 20%.**
- **Ball targeting in wild doubles ALREADY WORKED — nothing to add.** The Shadow-era selector (`WotHandleInputChooseBallTarget`, `gBattleStruct->wotBallTarget`) is gated only on "both opponent slots alive", not on trainer/shadow, and `GetBallThrowableState` had already dropped vanilla's BALL_THROW_UNABLE_TWO_MONS block. Wild doubles inherit it for free.
- **Draco darts north after the starter battle** (`Munen_village_2_Movement_HaruLeave`: 8x `walk_fast_down` -> `walk_fast_left` + 7x `walk_fast_up`). **The sidestep west is load-bearing:** he ends the approach at (25,5) deliberately "just south of the player" at (25,4), and applymovement never collision-checks, so running straight up would walk him through the player. Verified against the layout bin that x=24 is walkable from y=5 to y=0.
- **Starters get a signature STAB each**, replacing filler rather than Dragon Breath: **Gible -> THOUSAND_ARROWS** (Zygarde's, 90 BP Ground, uniquely hits Flying — Gible had no Ground move at all), **Frigibax -> GLACIATE** (Kyurem's, 65 BP Ice, drops Speed), **Axew -> DRAGON_DARTS** (Dragapult's, 50 BP twice). These are strong for level 5 by design; dial back if the opening plays too easy.
- **Battles run at 8x while fast forward is on** (field stays 3x). `gMain.inBattle` picks the multiplier in `WotSkipThisVBlank`. **The battle intro is ACCELERATED, NOT SKIPPED** — it is a controller handshake state machine and jumping states risks a battle that never returns control, whereas speeding the clock cannot deadlock it. The ball cast and cries fly past in a blink; if Joe wants them genuinely elided rather than fast, that is a separate, riskier change to `DoBattleIntro`.

### 2026-09-08 (round 29) — doubles, 1/256 shinies, gold shiny box, Exp Share, 3x fast forward, big white Gengar (BUILT green, untested, UNCOMMITTED)
- **REGRESSION IN JOE'S PORYMAP SAVE — NOT reverted, needs his call.** `data/maps/Route_2/map.json` lost **7 of the 8** `Route_2_EventScript_AllisonNotice` coord triggers; only `(10,38)` survives. Those triggers exist because `CheckPathBetweenTrainerAndPlayer` walks the tiles between trainer and player, so **a water-bound trainer can never sight a player on land** — the line of triggers along the shoreline IS the fix for the bug Joe reported. With one trigger she only notices the player on that exact tile.
- **Double wild battles:** `B_DOUBLE_WILD_CHANCE 0 -> 10` (%). Also `B_DOUBLE_WILD_REQUIRE_2_MONS FALSE -> TRUE` — **required**, or the opening hours hand a lone starter a 2v1.
- **Shinies 1/256:** `SHINY_ODDS 131 -> 256` (`include/constants/pokemon.h`; the value is out of 65536, so 256 = 1/256; vanilla 8, this hack was ~1/500).
- **A shiny's HP/EXP box is gold.** `src/battle_interface.c`: `WotShinyHealthboxPalette()` derives a gold copy of the healthbox palette at runtime (no second .pal to drift), `WotSetHealthboxShiny()` points the box's TWO sprites at it (`healthboxSpriteId` is the LEFT half and carries the right half's id in `oam.affineParam` — miss that and the box is half gold). Applied from `UpdateHealthboxAttribute`, **not** at creation, because the box outlives the mon standing in it and a switch has to take the gold away again. **The health BAR is deliberately untouched** — it is a separate sprite on `TAG_HEALTHBAR_PAL`, so its green/yellow/red readout survives; tinting it too would trade a status signal for decoration. If no OBJ palette slot is free the box just stays normal rather than stealing one.
- **Exp Share, in the bag from the start and toggleable:** `I_EXP_SHARE_ITEM GEN_5 -> GEN_6` (becomes a Key Item that toggles), and `I_EXP_SHARE_FLAG` moved off `FLAG_SYS_POKEMON_GET` — that flag sets when the player first owns a Pokemon and **never clears**, which is fine for "always on" and useless as a switch — onto new **`FLAG_WOT_EXP_SHARE_ON = 0x4A8`**. Granted + switched on in `NewGameInitData`.
- **FLAG BOOKKEEPING CORRECTED: 0x2B0 was NOT free** despite what this doc said — it is `FLAG_WOT_HIDEOUT_REVEAL_DONE`. Everything through **0x2BB is claimed**, and 0x2BC+ are real vanilla flags. Free flags live in the **0x4A8-0x4AF** block (0x4A5-0x4A7 are taken by the Distortion World mega stones). **Next free: 0x4A9.** Compute this instead of trusting a note: list `#define FLAG_UNUSED_0x*`, list the names aliased ONTO those, and diff.
- **BUG FIXED — the starting bag was never actually filled.** `NewGameInitData` called `AddBagItem(ITEM_RARE_CANDY/ITEM_QUEST_LOG)` at line ~193 and then `ClearBag()` at ~215, which `CpuFastFill`s the entire bag struct to zero. **The Quest Log has never been in a new game's bag.** All starting items now go in after `ClearBag()`.
- **3x fast forward that does not touch the audio** (`src/main.c`). One pass of `AgbMainLoop` is one LOGIC frame, and **the audio is not driven from it at all**: `m4aSoundMain()` runs in `VBlankIntr` and `m4aSoundVSync()` in `VCountIntr`, both on the hardware's interrupt schedule. So running the logic 3x between two VBlanks speeds the game up with the music untouched — no resampling, no pitch shift. **Only the WAIT is skipped, never the work**, so no frame is stepped over. `PlayTimeCounter_Update()` and `MapMusicMain()` stay at one call per DISPLAYED frame — they measure real time, and ticking them per logic frame would run the clock and every music fade at 3x, which is the exact thing this must not do. **Disabled during link play** (`gReceivedRemoteLinkPlayers` / `gLinkTransferringData`) — two linked games must agree on elapsed frames. Known degradation: three logic frames feed one VBlank's DMA queue, and `RequestDma3Copy` returns -1 when its 128 slots fill, so a heavy frame may DROP a graphics update at 3x. It cannot corrupt anything.
- **THE OPTIONS LIST SCROLLS NOW.** Round 28 filled the panel to all 20 tile rows, so a ninth option could not be added the same way and the 15px font ink forbids a tighter pitch. `YPOS_*` keep their names but become **runtime** positions, `ROW_Y(item) = (item - sScroll) * 16` — so **every existing `*_DrawChoices` call site kept working untouched**. `DrawOptionMenuChoice` early-returns when `y >= VISIBLE_ROWS * 16`; y is a `u8`, so a row scrolled above the window arrives as a large number (-16 -> 240) and one below as >= 128, and that single unsigned test catches both. Verified: 10 items, 8 visible, cursor in-window at every selection.
- **Big white Gengar in the Distortion World.** `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_SPECIES_SHINY(GENGAR)` -> `..._SHINY(GENGAR_MEGA)`; the shiny Mega palette is greyscale, which is the white. `graphics/pokemon/gengar/mega/overworld.png` upscaled 2x with NEAREST (192x32 -> 384x64, six frames, the same convention Steelix uses) and the species' `OVERWORLD(...)` size `SIZE_32x32 -> SIZE_64x64`. `OW_BATTLE_ONLY_FORMS` was already TRUE. **Caveat:** this is the species-wide entry, so any Mega Gengar FOLLOWER would also be giant — harmless in practice, since megas revert outside battle.
- **Save layout re-verified after adding two option bits** (portraits + fast forward, 14 of the u16's 16 bits now used): 3908 / 15572, identical to the shipped v1.2.1 ROM. `scratchpad/verify_saveblock.py`.
- Joe's own edits left alone: `wild_encounters.json` level ranges, `Munen_village_2/map.bin`, `snow_and_stone/metatiles.bin`, `YachtInterior/map.json`.

### 2026-09-08 (round 28) — PORTRAITS on/off in the Options menu (BUILT green, untested)
- **New option row "PORTRAITS" (ON/OFF) in the in-game Options menu**, reachable from the start menu at any time, exactly as asked. Default ON.
- **STORAGE COSTS NOTHING AND CANNOT BREAK SAVES.** The `u16` bitfield at `SaveBlock2 + 0x14` already had **four spare bits** reserved as `//u16 padding1:4`; `optionsPortraitsOff:1` takes one, leaving `padding1:3`. 12 bits were used, 13 now — still one `u16`. **Verified empirically, not assumed:** the GF ROM header at `0x100` publishes `sizeof(SaveBlock2)`/`sizeof(SaveBlock1)` (offsets `+136`/`+140`), and reading those out of the new build and out of the SHIPPED v1.2.1 ROM (reconstructed by applying `docs/patches/wishes-of-tomorrow-1.2.1.bps` to the retail base) gives **3908 / 15572 in both**. A fresh bit also reads 0 on every existing save, which is "portraits on". Script: `scratchpad/verify_saveblock.py` — **reuse this whenever a save-affecting struct is touched.**
- **ONE choke point covers every portrait.** `ShowNpcPortrait` in `src/npc_portrait.c` is reached by both the scripted `showportrait` command (`scrcmd.c`) and the automatic speaker portraits (`WotShowSpeakerPortrait` <- `field_name_box.c`), so a single early-return there is the whole feature. The option is read **live** on each call, so switching it off takes effect on the very next line of dialogue rather than at the next map load — that is what makes it "changeable at any point".
- **NOT gated (deliberate):** `boss_intro.c` calls `GetNpcPortraitGfx` to build the boss card (General Edwards / Siren Allison banners). That is a boss banner, not a dialogue portrait, and Joe has always called it a banner. Flagged to him; one line to include if he wants it.
- **THE LAYOUT WAS EXACTLY FULL — this is the real work.** `WIN_OPTIONS` was `tilemapTop 5, height 14` = 112 px, and 7 rows x 16 px = 112. An 8th row would have been clipped off entirely.
  - **Tightening the pitch to 14 px does NOT work.** Measured `graphics/fonts/latin_normal.png`: FONT_NORMAL's ink is **15 px tall** (rows 0..14 of each 16 px cell), so a 14 px row clips every line. `maxLetterHeight = 16` in the font table is the cell, not the ink — measure the sheet, do not trust the metric.
  - **The two extra tile rows came from merging the header's frame box and the list's frame box into ONE panel.** Two boxes spend four tile rows on frame; the screen has twenty, and eight 16 px options need sixteen. Header window itself does not move (still rows 1-2). New rows: 0 frame-top, 1-2 header, **3-18 options (16 rows)**, 19 frame-bottom = exactly 20.
  - `WIN_OPTIONS` -> `tilemapTop 3, height 16`; `HighlightOptionMenuItem` offset `index*16 + 40/56` -> `+ 24/40` (24 px = tile row 3).
- **BUG CAUGHT BEFORE SHIPPING — BG tile-space collision.** The window frame graphics are loaded with `LoadBgTiles(1, ..., 0x120, 0x1A2)`. **`0x1A2` = 418 was exactly one past the end of the old window** (`0x36` + 26*14 = 418) — not a coincidence, the frame was packed right behind it. Growing the window to 26*16 takes it to tile **469**, straight over the border graphics, which would have rendered the frame as garbage. Fixed by deriving the base from the window: `#define TILE_FRAME_BASE (0x36 + 26 * 16)` = `0x1D6` (470), with the eight corner/edge tiles as offsets from it, so a future resize cannot silently corrupt the frame again. Frame ends at 478 of the char block's 512 (33 spare). **The defines had to move ABOVE `CB2_InitOptionMenu`** — `LoadBgTiles` uses them at line ~222 while they lived at ~694.
- **Static geometry check (scratchpad, worth repeating for any menu resize):** header tiles 2..53, options 54..469, frame 470..478 — no overlaps; 8 rows at 16 px pitch need 128 px against a 128 px window; highlight row 0 = 24..40 and row 7 = 136..152 inside a 24..152 window.
- **The boss card is gated too, at Joe's request — but ONLY the portrait.** `src/boss_intro.c` sets `gSprites[tSprPort].invisible = gSaveBlock2Ptr->optionsPortraitsOff` right after creating the sprite. The nameplate keeps its entire performance: slide, slam, name reveal, shimmer, white pop and tint are all untouched. **The sprite is still CREATED and still slides even when hidden** — `BI_STATE_EXIT` waits on `gSprites[tSprPort].x >= BI_PORTRAIT_X_OFF` before the card may finish, and `BossIntro_FlashCardWhite`/`_RestoreCardPalettes`/`_FadeMask` all address `TAG_BI_PORTRAIT`. Skipping creation would strand the exit state and unbalance the tint mask; hiding it changes nothing but the drawing.
- Files: `include/global.h` (the bit), `src/new_game.c` (default off = portraits on), `src/npc_portrait.c` (the gate), `src/option_menu.c` (row, handlers, window, frame, tile base). **Uncommitted** — Joe has not asked to commit.

### 2026-08-28 (round 27) — site: screenshots become a rail, changelog becomes bullets (site only, no ROM change)
- **Eight new media assets** built from `custom ui/Website Screenshots/hackdex/` into `docs/assets/media/`. Stills are upscaled **4x with NEAREST** to 960x640, matching the existing assets and guaranteeing crisp pixels whatever a browser does with `image-rendering: pixelated`. Animations stay at the GBA's native **240x160** — a 4x animated webp of 127 frames is enormous for no gain, and 240x160 is the same 3:2 the tile's `aspect-ratio` expects, so layout is identical either way. Total for all eight: **247 KB**.
- **`summit-reveal.webp` was REPLACED with the censored capture** (the leader's name is redacted in-game art), as asked.
- **`doubles.png` was deliberately NOT added** — compared against the existing `doubles.webp` and it is the same shot, just the native-resolution source of it. Adding it would have put the same battle on screen twice.
- **WEBP ANIMATION GOTCHA:** PIL reports `duration = 0` for every frame of an animated webp on read-back, and coalesces identical consecutive frames (82 source frames of the near-static title screen became 6). Neither is a fault — parsing the container's **ANMF chunks** (24-bit LE duration at payload offset 12) shows the totals are preserved exactly: 2740 / 4250 / 3240 ms, identical to the source GIFs. **Verify webp timing by parsing ANMF, never by PIL read-back.**
- **The screenshot grid became a rail**, sharing `.cast-rail`'s scroller rather than getting its own: same gesture handling, same snap, one place to fix it. 13 tiles. Verified in-browser at 375x812 and desktop: tile is 78% of a phone screen so the next peeks in, snap `x`/`start` engages (a set to 600 lands at 639), the wheel handler moves the rail horizontally, and **at the far end the wheel is NOT intercepted** so the page scrolls on normally. `document.scrollWidth == clientWidth` at mobile width — the page itself never scrolls sideways.
- **`[data-cast-rail]` became `[data-rail]`** and the JS now loops over all of them; it previously used `querySelector` (singular) and would have ignored the second rail entirely.
- **Marquee gained RANDOMIZER MODE.** It is duplicated twice in the markup to make the loop seamless — **both copies must change or the track jumps halfway through**.
- **Changelog rewritten as short bullets** for all four versions, casual, with the detail left to GitHub. `.change p` styling replaced with `.change ul/li`.
- **CACHE-BUST TOKEN — the site's most dangerous footgun.** `index.html` and `labs.html` load `styles.css`, `site.js` and `patcher.js` with a shared `?v=YYYYMMDDx` query. **Changing any of those three files without bumping the token ships nothing to a returning visitor** — the browser serves its cached copy under the unchanged URL. Caught live: the screenshot rail was rendering as a 13-tile vertical stack because the page still had the old CSS (`display: block`, `overflow-x: visible`). **The v1.2.1 release round had the same defect and it was missed** — patcher.js was rewritten to point at the 1.2.1 patch, but a returning visitor would have kept the 1.2.0 patcher. Token bumped `20260827a` -> `20260828b` across all 5 references. **Bump it in the same commit as any asset change; a direct `curl` of the bare asset URL will look correct and prove nothing, because the page requests a different cache key.**
- **SPLICE GOTCHA (cost a wasted pass):** `s.index("      </div>")` matches INSIDE `"        </div>"` — six spaces are a substring of eight. The changelog splice ended at the first entry and silently left the rest of the old list behind, which only surfaced as a div-balance check (121 open / 122 close). **Anchor a line-oriented splice on `eol + indent + tag`, never the bare tag.** Tag-balance counts before and after are the cheap way to catch it: 115/115 div, 8/8 section, 14/14 figure, 4/4 ul.

### 2026-08-28 (round 26) — v1.2.1 hotfix released; site bumped; screenshots rewritten out of history
- **v1.2.1 = the hotfix round + the title screen.** Game commits since the v1.2.0 pointer (`6831f26867`): `fc985e5472` (Shadow mega evolve, Shadow Jirachi at the summit, mega forms on the champion/resume screens, the end-game ferry, Siren Allison) and `985b416636` (the composited title screen). 33 files, +1422 / -76.
- **SAVE-COMPATIBLE, and this was verified rather than assumed** before the site was allowed to promise it: `git diff 6831f26867..HEAD` touches none of `global.h`, `pokemon.h`, `save.h`, `save.c`, `flags.h`, `vars.h`, and contains zero `struct SaveBlock` changes.
- **Patch: `docs/patches/wishes-of-tomorrow-1.2.1.bps`, 22,736,706 bytes.** Base CRC32 `1F1C08FB` (the same retail dump every release has used), **new output CRC32 `81AF35BA`**. Built by `Tools/make_bps_patch.py`, which now **takes the version on the command line** (`python Tools/make_bps_patch.py 1.2.1`) instead of needing three hardcoded paths edited each release. Verified the established way: an independent applier, written from the BPS spec and sharing no code with the encoder, reproduces the built ROM byte-for-byte and all three embedded CRCs check.
- **Site bumped 1.2.0 -> 1.2.1** across five files: `assets/patcher.js` (url + outputName), `assets/site.js` (version), `index.html` (hero chip, download heading, release-asset href, new changelog entry dated 28 Aug, save-compat FAQ), `labs.html` (project chip), `patches/README.md` (current-patch table + new CRC, 1.2.0 moved to the archived list, the encoder note). 1.2.0's bps and its release asset are untouched — the download counter sums across ALL releases.
- **GOTCHA — the site files are CRLF.** A multi-line search/replace written with `\n` silently matches nothing. Read with `newline=""`, detect the file's own ending, and translate every pattern to it before matching (single-line patterns are unaffected, which is why the first three files succeeded and the next two did not).
- **The screenshot commits were rewritten out of history and force-pushed**, on Joe's instruction that the repo carries the game and what builds it — not marketing material. Both branches were reset and recommitted; `git ls-tree -r HEAD` on `main` now shows zero screenshot/mp4 paths, and the game repo tracks its own `Website Screenshots/` exactly as it did before, with Joe's local move still uncommitted. **Force-push used `--force-with-lease`.**

### 2026-08-28 (round 25) — the title screen is COMPOSITED from layers; the extractor is gone (BUILT green 13:27, untested)
- **Joe supplied the art as four separate layers, and that deleted the entire problem.** Rounds 17–24 were all one fight: a single flattened mock-up came in, and the tool had to cut the lettering and Jirachi back OUT of it before it could put new ones in — mask by colour, then inpaint the hole. That job is unwinnable on this art, for two independent reasons:
  - **no colour test separates Jirachi's silver from the mountain's shaded snow** — they are the same neutral grey, which is what made the mask carve 40-row holes down the peak;
  - **no fill invents stars.** Any interpolation returns a smooth ramp, and a smooth ramp surrounded by real starfield reads as a washed pale band. Five different fills failed on this and the fifth (mirroring real pixels) only made it *acceptable*.
  Both problems vanish when the layers arrive separately. There is nothing to detect, nothing to cut out, and no hole to fill.
- **`tools/build_title_screen.py` is a full rewrite: 812 lines -> 331, a compositor rather than an extractor.** Deleted outright, all of it now dead weight: `sky_fill`, `diffusion_fill`, `inpaint`, `mirror_sky_up`, `mirror_sky_sideways`, `remove_jirachi_aura`, `replace_jirachi`, `replace_subtitle`, `looks_like_sky`, `grow`, `text_mask`/`draw_version`/`draw_version_into`/`version_tones`/`VERSION_FONT`, and every `BAKE_*`/`REMOVE_AURA`/`REPLACE_JIRACHI` toggle.
- **The layer stack** (bottom to top), all from `custom ui/`, each simply alpha-composited over the one below:

  | layer | source | size | lands at |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | background | `start menu full bg.jpg` | 240x160 | full frame |
  | logo | `pokemon-logo.png` | 140x51 | x 50..189, y 1..51 |
  | title lockup | `wishes text.jpg` | 113x18 | x 63..175, y 52..69 |
  | Jirachi | `jirachi.png` | 32x34 | x 104..135, y 71..104 |

  Placement was **measured, not guessed**: the approved mock-up's logo occupied y 8..51, and the new background's snow cap starts at y 93 with its apex near x 120 — so Jirachi hovers just above the peak, as drawn.
- **`start menu full bg.jpg` is clean** — no logo, no lettering, no Jirachi, and no PRESS START. Nothing is painted out of the background any more.
- **PRESS START is still NOT composited, on purpose.** It blinks, so it is a sprite, and a sprite must not also sit in the picture underneath or a permanent copy shows through the blink. Its lettering is still lifted from `start menu revised.jpg`, the only place the artist ever drew it: 576 px, 15 tones, into a 32x64 four-frame stacked sheet (frame 3 empty — the version number is gone, and the C only creates three sprites). Unchanged on the C side: 3 sprites at x 88/120/152, y 139, blinking on `sWotTextBlinkTimer & 32`.
- **Kept from the earlier rounds, because these were the parts that were actually right:** the grid-rejecting per-cell median downscale (a median throws the baked canvas grid away as a minority sample without needing its period, phase, colour or opacity); no dithering (Floyd–Steinberg speckle reads as flicker on a static screen); 255-colour quantise with slot 0 left free for the backdrop. Output is 600 unique tiles / 38,400 bytes at 8bpp — the worst case, and what shipped before.
- **Layers alpha-blend rather than key hard.** At 255 colours there is room for the handful of in-between tones, and they are what stop the lockup and Jirachi looking cut out with scissors. `premultiplied_resize` still multiplies by alpha before resampling and divides back out, so no transparent-pixel colour bleeds into a rim.
- **RULE (the general lesson of rounds 17–25): when artwork has to be recombined, ask the artist for LAYERS before writing a single line of detection code.** Segmenting a flattened composite is guesswork that degrades the art; compositing separate layers is exact and needs no cleverness at all. Every artifact Joe reported across nine rounds — grid, speckle, halo, washed band, pale streaks, eaten snow — was a symptom of extraction, and none of them survived the switch.
- **COMMITTED AND PUSHED (2026-08-28).** Game `master`: **`985b416636`** (composite the title screen) on top of `fc985e5472` (the hotfix round). Project `main`: the commit carrying this handoff, bumping the submodule to `985b416636`. **Both remotes are the SAME GitHub repo** — `Emerald-Rom-Hack` now redirects to `Wishes-Game`; the project lives on `main`, the game source on `master`.
- **What is deliberately NOT tracked — check here before assuming something was lost:**
  - **Screenshots and video.** `custom ui/Website Screenshots/` (81 MB, of which 77 MB is four `.mp4` files) stays untracked, by Joe's instruction: the repo carries the game and what builds it, not marketing material. An earlier pass committed 30 of those files and moved 23 out of the game repo; **both were rewritten out of history and force-pushed**, so the blobs never landed. The game repo therefore still tracks its own `Website Screenshots/`, and Joe's working-tree move of them remains an uncommitted local change.
  - **`custom ui/start menu.jpg`** — the first flattened mock-up, superseded and read by nothing. `start menu revised.jpg` IS tracked, because the generator still lifts the PRESS START lettering out of it.
  - **`agbcc` (597 files "modified")** is a `100755 -> 100644` permission flip from the Windows checkout, NOT an edit. Committing it would strip the executable bit off `build.sh` and 596 others and break the build on Linux/macOS. Same for `pokeemerald-expansion/.github/docs_validate/inclusive_summary.py`. **Never `git add -A` at either repo root** — stage explicit paths. (The `core.filemode` hazard noted below, in a new disguise.)
- **Repeat gotcha:** a large Python file written through a bash heredoc failed on quoting again (`unexpected EOF while looking for matching '`). Use the Write tool for anything of that size; noted previously and hit anyway.

### 2026-08-28 (round 24) — the sky behind the title is REBUILT, not interpolated (BUILT green 10:45, untested)
- **Joe's diagnosis was the right one: reconstruct the background first, paste the text and Jirachi on top.** The architecture was already that; the reconstruction was the weak part, and interpolation of any flavour was never going to be enough. **Nothing you interpolate can contain stars.** A 110x22 hole comes back as a smooth ramp, and against real starfield either side that reads as the pale washed band Joe kept seeing.
- **The fix is to MIRROR real sky into the hole instead of computing one.** Two holes, two directions, chosen by which axis is short:
  - **the subtitle band** (110 wide, 22 tall) is rebuilt by reflecting the clean sky from the ~16 rows BELOW it upward, per column, so the vertical gradient, the banding and the stars all come with it;
  - **Jirachi's smoke** (tall, narrow, clean nebula either side at every height) is rebuilt by reflecting sideways, per row.
- **Four earlier attempts and exactly why each failed** -- worth keeping, because each looked reasonable:
  1. inverse-distance in all four directions -- reached down to the mountain and dragged a pale column of snow up into the sky;
  2. a per-row ramp between the single pixels either side -- no texture, so a flat pale band;
  3. copying whole runs of pixels along the row -- the hole is 110px wide and there is no 110px of clean sky beside it, so it imported ROCKS and FLAMES into the sky;
  4. per-column vertical interpolation -- for the middle columns the Pokemon logo sits directly above the hole, so it either streaked logo yellow downward or, once yellow was rejected, reached over the logo to the dark navy at the top of the screen and ramped THAT down.
- **A separate bug found on the way: the Jirachi `body` mask was eating the mountain.** Jirachi's silver is neutral grey and so is the peak's SHADED snow -- indistinguishable by colour. The mask now has two boxes: the body may only be removed in the band above the snow line, while the (much tighter) smoke mask may run lower.
- **RULE: to repair a hole in textured art, reflect real neighbouring pixels into it across its SHORT axis. Interpolation is only ever right for a hole in a smooth gradient.**
- A few small purple flecks of the old aura survive around Jirachi. They read as part of the design; tightening further starts eating the figure.
- TEST CHECKLIST: the sky behind the subtitle has stars and banding like the rest of the sky, with no pale band; no rectangular halo around Jirachi; no yellow or navy bleeding down from the logo; the peak is unstreaked.

### 2026-08-28 (round 23) — new Shadow Jirachi on the title; the sky fill stops smearing (BUILT green 10:31, untested)
- **The drawn Jirachi and its smoke are replaced wholesale** by `custom sprites/shadowjirachi_fb1.png` -- a 64x128 green-keyed sheet holding a FRONT frame (y4-56) and a back frame. The front one goes on the title at **31x33** centred on (120,86), a little larger than the ~26px figure it replaces. It brings its own purple streamers, so the old aura-masking is superseded: the smoke leaves with the figure rather than being picked off it. `REPLACE_JIRACHI = False` restores the drawn one.
- **GREEN FRINGE, and the fix.** Resizing the sprite with the key still in place blends green into every edge pixel -- a lurid halo all round it. The key is cut to real transparency FIRST, then the colour is **premultiplied by alpha**, resized, and divided back out, because resampling RGBA naively blends the now-transparent pixels' colour in instead. Same trick as Allison's portrait; it is the standard answer whenever keyed art is scaled.
- **THE SLOPPY BLUR IS GONE -- and the cause was the fill sweeping VERTICALLY.** `sky_fill` now reads along ROWS only. The sky's gradient runs vertically, so every row already holds the right colour for its own height, and filling a hole from the pixels either side of it on the SAME row carries that gradient straight through along with the nebula's banding. The previous inverse-distance version also swept up and down, which above Jirachi reached all the way to the mountain snow and dragged a pale column up into the sky; that was the wash. `diffusion_fill` is kept but no longer used for these.
- **RULE: to close a hole in a gradient, fill ALONG the gradient's flat axis, not across it.** Averaging in both axes turns any large hole into a smear of everything around it.
- TEST CHECKLIST: Jirachi has no green edging at 1x; the sky under the subtitle and around Jirachi is continuous with no pale band or blur; Jirachi sits on the peak without clipping the snow oddly; PRESS START still blinks.

### 2026-08-28 (round 22) — title tweaks: bigger stroked subtitle, no version, blink back (BUILT green 10:14, untested)
- **Subtitle: the box is gone.** The bar (border and shadow included) is filled in and the lockup now sits straight on the sky at **124x18** -- against 97x12 in the bar -- in white with a **one-pixel black stroke**, which is the only thing holding it off a busy nebula now the dark box is gone. `SUBTITLE_HEIGHT` / `SUBTITLE_STRETCH` / `SUBTITLE_CENTRE_Y` tune it.
- **New `diffusion_fill()` for the big holes.** The ring-average inpaint is fine for a few pixels of text on a flat slope, but the bar leaves a hole 110x22 and averaging that from its rim gives one flat smear. Relaxing it instead -- every unknown pixel repeatedly taking the mean of its known neighbours -- reconstructs the sky gradient that runs through the hole.
- **Version number removed** entirely (`DRAW_VERSION = False`); its glyphs are painted out and nothing is drawn back.
- **PRESS START blinks again.** It is a sprite once more, so it is painted OUT of the background (`BAKE_PRESS_START = False`) -- a baked copy underneath a blinking sprite would show through. Only the three PRESS START pieces are created; sheet frame 3 (the version) is now unused.
- **Jirachi's aura: reduced, not eliminated, and that is deliberate.** The silver body is found first and dilated by a pixel so the fill cannot eat Jirachi's own dark outline along with the smoke. **A wider mask was tried and looked WORSE:** the aura is soft smoke shading continuously into the sky, so there is no clean edge; take more of it and the fill can only interpolate across a big hole, giving a grey blur that reads as a cloud. The tight core mask (476 px) leaves a faint rim that reads as a soft shadow, and Jirachi is far clearer. Three variants were composited and compared before choosing. `REMOVE_AURA = False` restores the original smoke. **A version of the art drawn without the aura would beat any of this.**
- **PALETTE HEADROOM, asked and answered: the BG palette is FULL -- 255 of 256 slots carry image data, 0 free.** Nothing can be cycled there without taking a colour off the picture. **The OBJ palette is separate and nearly empty**: 256 more colours, of which the title uses about three banks (version, press-start, title-text). So **animation on this screen wants to be sprites**, which is exactly how PRESS START blinks. If a background animation is specifically wanted, quantising the picture to ~240 colours would free 15 BG slots to cycle, at a small and probably invisible cost.
- TEST CHECKLIST: subtitle legible at 1x with its stroke intact; no trace of the old bar; PRESS START blinks and nothing shows underneath it; no version number anywhere; Jirachi reads clearly.

### 2026-08-28 (round 21) — stylised subtitle lockup in the title bar (BUILT green 09:28, untested)
- **The drawn "WISHES OF TOMORROW" is replaced by Joe's ornate lockup** (`custom ui/wishes text.jpg`, white on black so it keys on luminance alone). The old glyphs are painted out of the bar's dark interior and the new lettering drawn in its place; the light purple border is untouched.
- **It is STRETCHED to the bar's width, deliberately.** The lockup's aspect is **5.85** against the drawn lettering's ~10, so at the bar's true 12-row interior it would come out only 70px wide -- thin, mushy and adrift in a mostly empty bar. All three options were composited into the real bar and compared: true aspect looked lost, and the full-width version both fills the bar the way the original lettering did and reads the most clearly. `SUBTITLE_FILL_WIDTH = False` in the tool reverts to true aspect.
- **The bar's colours are sampled, not hard-coded** -- a dark pixel for the interior fill and a light one for the ink -- so a re-export with a different palette still matches. There is a guard that skips the whole step if the box no longer looks like a dark bar, rather than painting over the picture.
- `SUBTITLE_BOX = (70, 54, 170, 65)` is the bar's dark interior, **measured off the rendered 240x160 picture**, not guessed: the interior is exactly 12 rows (y54-65), with the border outside it and the old glyphs at y55-64.
- TEST CHECKLIST: the subtitle reads cleanly at 1x on hardware; the purple border is intact and unshifted; nothing else on the title changed.

### 2026-08-28 (round 20) — THE FLICKER FOUND: TwinkleStars was eating the logo's palette (BUILT green 08:50, untested)
- **Joe called it: the old title's star twinkle was still running.** `TwinkleStars()` is called from the title screen's `MainCB2` EVERY FRAME and writes `gPlttBufferFaded[BG_PLTT_ID(bank) + 12 + ch] = RGB(v, v, v)` for `STAR_BG_BANKS` (3) banks x `NUM_STAR_CHANNELS` (4) -- **twelve BG palette entries: 12-15, 28-31 and 44-47.**
- **Under the old 4bpp title those were spare slots at the top of each 16-colour bank. The title is now ONE 256-colour palette, so they are ordinary colours of the picture.** Verified against the generated tiles rather than assumed: the logo region uses **seven** of the twelve, and **five of those are its yellows** -- (253,208,18), (253,204,11), (251,204,11), (252,201,14), (248,199,22) -- all being replaced with a random grey sixty times a second. That is precisely why the logo would not sit solid yellow and why pixels appeared to flicker.
- **Call removed** (function kept, marked UNUSED). The stars are painted into the art anyway.
- **RULE, and this is the second time it has bitten in two rounds:** moving a background from 4bpp to 8bpp turns every "spare slot at the top of a bank" into a real image colour. `UpdateLegendaryMarkingColor` (entry 239) was the same class of bug in round 17. **Anything that writes a fixed BG palette index on a 256-colour screen is now writing over the picture.**
- **Joe's revised art is in and wired up:** `custom ui/start menu revised.jpg`. Sub-cell modulation measured at **7.8 levels against the old file's 15.4**, i.e. the canvas grid is genuinely gone from the source; the per-cell median takes the remainder. `SRC` in `tools/build_title_screen.py` now points at it.
- TEST CHECKLIST: the Pokemon logo is flat yellow and stays flat; nothing anywhere on the title moves; no mesh in the sky; PRESS START and v1.2.0 read cleanly; A/START still reaches the main menu.

- **Every moving part is gone.** No blink, no Jirachi bob, no logo shine, no BG scroll -- and **no sprites at all**. The title is now one 8bpp background drawn once. Joe asked for this to isolate a "fragmenting" report in the live test, and it removes a whole class of cause at a stroke: with nothing composited over the picture there is no sprite-over-background seam left to tear.
- **PRESS START and the version are baked into the background.** PRESS START is left EXACTLY as the artist drew it -- painting it out and redrawing it could only lose fidelity. The version cannot be left alone, because the art reads v2.0.0 and the game is v1.2.0, so that line alone is painted out and `v1.2.0` drawn back in the tones lifted from the original glyphs.
- **Reversible in one line:** `BAKE_TEXT = False` in `tools/build_title_screen.py` goes back to painting the text out, and `CreateWotTitleText()` (kept, marked UNUSED) puts the blinking sprites back. The sprite sheet is still generated either way.
- `StartPokemonLogoShine` is disabled at its own entry point rather than at its four call sites -- it swept across where the stock logo used to be, and there is no stock logo any more.
- **STILL OUTSTANDING: Joe's revised, grid-free JPG never reached the disk.** `custom ui/start menu.jpg` is still the old file (checked: same 1259x848, same 15.4 levels of sub-cell modulation, i.e. the grid is still in it). The faint mesh remaining in the sky is that old source, not the pipeline. Once the new art is saved to that path, `python tools/build_title_screen.py` regenerates everything -- no code changes needed.
- TEST CHECKLIST: nothing on the title moves at all; PRESS START and v1.2.0 read cleanly and are part of the picture; A/START still reaches the main menu; confirm whether the "fragmenting" survives with all sprites gone -- if it does, the cause is in the background load itself and not in compositing.

### 2026-08-28 (round 18) — title screen sharpening: dither out, canvas grid out (BUILT green 02:34, untested)
- **THE "FLICKERING PIXELS" WERE FLOYD-STEINBERG DITHER.** Joe reported pixels flickering on the Pokemon logo and in the rocks. Dither fakes a third colour by chequering two others; on a STATIC screen that chequer reads as shimmer, and it is also why nothing looked solid. **Quantising with `dither=Image.NONE`** fixed the logo, the rocks and the flatness complaint in one move -- with 255 colours available there is nothing to fake.
- **THE CANVAS GRID TOOK FOUR ATTEMPTS. Three plausible models were all wrong:**
  1. *A dark line every 21 source px.* No -- 21 is FOUR lines. The true period is one drawn pixel: 1259/240 = **5.2458 source px**.
  2. *Repaint the darkest line, phase found from a global profile.* The phase came out wrong for rows because the picture's own content dominates the fold; half the mesh survived.
  3. *The lines sit on cell boundaries, so sample cell interiors.* **Measured, the darkest phase is 44% INTO the cell -- near its middle.** Interior sampling was aiming directly at the grid, which is why it scored WORSE on a 4-periodic test than plain LANCZOS.
- **What actually worked: a PER-CELL MEDIAN.** Each drawn pixel is a 5.25 x 5.25 block of source pixels and the grid crosses about one row and one column of it -- a **minority** of the samples, which a median discards by construction. No period, phase, colour or opacity has to be guessed correctly. It also returns a flat block exactly (solid colours) and does no cross-cell blending (sharp), so it answered all three of Joe's complaints at once. The median is then **snapped to a colour that genuinely occurs in the block**, so every output pixel is a colour the artist really used rather than a per-channel blend of three.
- **RULE worth keeping: to recover pixel art from an upscaled/JPEG'd export, take a per-cell MEDIAN, not a resample.** Averaging filters rake every contaminant -- grid lines, ringing, JPEG mosquito noise -- into every output pixel; a median rejects anything that is not the majority of the cell. The same idea is already in the Clarkson portrait pipeline ("median native extract") and in Allison's 2x halving (majority vote per 2x2).
- A measurement caveat that cost time: **a 4-periodic amplitude test cannot tell grid from art.** Sharper sampling preserves more real high-frequency detail and therefore scores higher, so the metric ranked the best method last. Judged by eye after that.
- TEST CHECKLIST: logo and rocks are flat colour with no shimmer; no mesh in the sky; edges crisp; PRESS START / v1.2.0 still blink together and sit exactly over their painted-out patch.

### 2026-08-27 (round 17) — NEW TITLE SCREEN from Joe's mock-up, at 256 colours (BUILT green 23:49, untested)
- **The whole title is now ONE 8bpp (256-colour) background**, generated from `custom ui/start menu.jpg` by `tools/build_title_screen.py`. 4bpp gives sixteen colours per 8x8 tile and visibly bands that nebula; at 8bpp the entire screen shares one 256-entry palette and the gradient survives. **600 unique tiles x 64 bytes = 38,400 bytes** at char base 0 (0x0000-0x95FF), tilemap at **screen base 30** (0xF000-0xF7FF) -- 23KB of clear air between them. The stock logo / Rayquaza / clouds loads were REMOVED, not just left unused: they target char bases 0 and 2 and screen base 9, all of which now sit inside the tileset and would have shredded it.
- **PRESS START and the version are SPRITES so they can blink**, and were therefore **painted out of the background art first** -- otherwise a static copy would sit under the blinking one. The inpaint is a widening ring average, which is invisible on that smooth mountain slope. Four stacked 32x16 frames: three of PRESS START (lifted straight from the mock-up) and one version string. **32x16 because the GBA has no 64x16 object** -- legal horizontal sizes are 16x8, 32x8, 32x16 and 64x32. All four blink off ONE shared counter (`sWotTextBlinkTimer`, ticked in `Task_TitleScreenPhase3`), or the three pieces of PRESS START would drift apart and flicker independently.
- **The version string is DRAWN, not lifted.** The mock-up reads v2.0.0 and the game is v1.2.0, and the mock-up's glyph outlines touch each other so there is no column gap to segment them on. A small blocky face in the tool draws `v1.2.0` with the outline derived by dilation, tinted from tones sampled out of the mock-up's own version line so it sits in the same palette.
- **TWO BUGS OF MY OWN, caught before they shipped:** (1) `LoadPalette(..., 2 * PLTT_SIZE_8BPP)` asks for 1024 bytes when BG palette RAM is 512 -- it would have run straight on into the OBJ palettes and wiped the text sprite's colours. One bank is the whole BG palette. (2) **`UpdateLegendaryMarkingColor` writes `BG_PLTT_ID(14) + 15` -- entry 239** -- a spare slot under the stock 4bpp layout but a real colour of the 256-colour art, so it would have left one shade of the picture pulsing red every frame. Call removed and the function deleted.
- **Jirachi is now baked into the background** and the bobbing OBJ sprite is no longer created -- the mock-up draws Jirachi in a fixed spot, so keeping the sprite would have shown two of it. **This loses the bob**; restoring it means inpainting Jirachi out of the nebula, which is much harder than lifting text off a flat slope. Joe's call.
- Assets: `graphics/title_screen/wot_title_bg.{png,bin,pal}` and `wot_title_text.png`. The old `wot_title.*` set is left in place but is no longer drawn. `.bin.smolTM` is generated by the build (`%.smolTM: %`) and is gitignored, so only the `.bin` is checked in.
- TEST CHECKLIST: the title reads as the mock-up with no banding in the sky; PRESS START and v1.2.0 blink together and in step; no stray colour pulses anywhere in the art; the sprite text sits exactly over where it was painted out (no ghost, no offset); A/START still reaches the main menu.

### 2026-08-27 (round 16) — Siren Allison: land detection, the swim-ashore approach, splashes (BUILT green 22:09, untested)
- **WHY SHE ONLY EVER CHALLENGED SURFERS -- it was structural, not a mistake.** She sits at `Route_2 (6,38)`, which is a **water tile (elevation 1)**, and `CheckPathBetweenTrainerAndPlayer` walks every tile between trainer and player and abandons the sighting on the first collision. A water-bound object is blocked the instant that path steps up onto the sand, so her sight line could never reach a player on the beach no matter what range or elevation she was given. **RULE: trainer sight can never cross a water/land boundary. Use coord triggers.**
- **Her detection is now split, deliberately.** Ordinary trainer sight still handles players out on the water, where it works. Seven **coord triggers on the walkable shore tiles** -- (12,35) (11,36) (11,37) (11,38) (11,39) (12,40) (13,41) -- handle players on the beach. **(10,38) is deliberately NOT a trigger**: that is where she comes ashore, and it is only reachable through (11,38), so she always fires before the player can be standing on it.
- **The approach.** She spots the player, swims three tiles east with **a splash on each stroke** (`SE_M_SURF` retriggered per tile -- one long SE would be finished before she arrived), comes out of the water on `SE_M_DIVE`, and takes a real step up onto the sand on `SE_PUDDLE`. **Scripted `applymovement` never collision-checks, which is the only reason she can cross the waterline at all.** The player still cannot walk on water: no collision data was touched.
- **The swim/walk sprite swap.** An object cannot be handed a new graphic in place, so her map object is now `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_VAR_0` and the scene does `removeobject` -> `setvar VAR_OBJ_GFX_ID_0` -> `setobjectxyperm` -> `addobject`. Her elevation is the **0 wildcard** so one object can legally stand on water during the swim and on sand after it. `Route_2_OnTransition` (the map had NO map scripts before) restores both the swimming graphic **and her template position** on every entry -- **object templates live in the SAVE BLOCK, so `setobjectxyperm` would otherwise creep her towards the beach a little further with every encounter.**
- **The banner shimmer.** Her boss-intro entry already carried the same `seCue = SE_M_DETECT` as Edwards -- the shimmer was being drowned, not missing. Both her scripts now run `WotSuppressWeatherSE` + `WotStopSceneSE` immediately before `bossintro`, which is exactly the fix Edwards needed; with splashes now playing during her approach that flush is doubly required.
- **ART DONE (Joe supplied the paths).** Sources: `custom sprites/Trainer Sprites/allison.png` for the overworld sheets and the battle sprite, `custom sprites/Key Trainer Sprites/swimmer_allison.png` for the portrait (Joe's second pick; `allison_2.jpg` was tried first and replaced). Two new reproducible tools: `tools/build_allison_sprites.py` and `tools/build_allison_art.py`.
- **The artist's sheet decoded:** one 416x192 image holding the tall battle figure on the left (content x52..117, y6..157) and, from x=160, an **8x4 grid of 32x48 cells**. Grid columns 0-3 are SWIMMING, 4-7 are WALKING; rows are down / left / right / up; each group of four columns is **stand, stepA, stand, stepB**, so the two distinct walk poses are columns 1 and 3 of the group. The right-facing row is unused -- the engine mirrors the left frames.
- **THE SOURCE SHEET IS 2x AND MUST BE HALVED -- shipped at full size she towered over every NPC on the route** (Joe's screenshot). Verified rather than assumed: on the (0,0) phase **98.9%% of its 2x2 blocks are one flat colour**, so it is genuinely double-scale and halving is all but lossless. The 1.1%% that are not uniform (the artist's own anti-aliasing) are resolved by **majority vote of the four pixels**, which keeps her outline from breaking up. Halved, a 32x48 cell becomes 16x24 and her walking figure 16x19 -- comfortably inside the **standard 16x32 NPC frame, the same size as the player**. Both sheets use it, so she does not change scale when the approach scene swaps swim for walk (the old pairing was 16x32 swim / 32x32 land).
- **Each halved cell is placed at a FIXED offset in its frame** rather than being individually re-centred, which preserves the artist's own frame-to-frame bob instead of flattening it.
- **Battle sprite and portrait rebuilt with a proper keying pipeline.** Magenta key -> **erode one pixel of fringe** -> **premultiplied LANCZOS** downscale -> 15 colours + transparent. **Two traps worth keeping:** (1) the sheet's background is palette INDEX 25 while index 0 is also black and is real outline, so keying on the colour would have eaten her outlines; (2) the portrait source's magenta key smears (around (247,3,236) on the PNG, (239,0,224) on the JPEG that was tried first), so a first attempt quantised it into an opaque near-magenta palette entry -- portraits treat slot 0 as transparent (cf. edwards.png / minister.png), so it has to be distance-keyed, not matched exactly.

- TEST CHECKLIST: her swimming and walking sprites are the same size and neither is clipped; walk the beach on foot and she notices, swims in with splashes, comes ashore and challenges; surf past her and the old sight path still works; her banner shimmer is audible in both; after the battle leave and re-enter Route 2 and confirm she is back out at (6,38) on the swimming sprite; confirm the player still cannot walk onto water.

### 2026-08-27 (round 15) — post-release hotfixes: Shadow Jirachi, mega display, the ferry (BUILT green 18:54, untested)
- **THE TOWER'S JIRACHI WAS THE WRONG JIRACHI.** `TowerTop` ran `setwildbattle SPECIES_JIRACHI, 70` + `special WotMakeEventMonShadow` -- i.e. an ORDINARY Jirachi wearing the Shadow flag, not `SPECIES_JIRACHI_SHADOW`. The custom species (Steel/Dark, its own front/back/icon/follower art, natDexNum JIRACHI) existed and was simply never spawned, which is why the caught mon reverted to a standard Jirachi in the post-game and on the save screen. Now spawns the real species.
- **Shadow status is no longer flag-only.** New `WotSpeciesIsShadow()` / `WotMonIsShadow()` in `wot_shadow_log.c`: the two story Shadow species (Jirachi, Lugia) are Shadow **by identity**, so a mon spawned or given without the flag still gets the aura announcement, the health-bar indicator and the heart gauge. Wired into `battle_gimmick.c` (indicator), `battle_util.c` (switch-in aura), `battle_interface.c` (heart gauge) and `wot_shadow_log.c` (MarkMon). **Purification still sticks:** a Shadow species that carries the National Ribbon reads as NOT Shadow, because purification clears the flag and the ribbon is the permanent record.
- **The violet tint no longer double-darkens custom Shadow art.** `battle_gfx_sfx_util.c` tinted anything with the Shadow flag; a species that is ALREADY drawn as a Shadow is now excluded, or its own art gets muddied on top of itself.
- **REGRESSION CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED: the Shadow Log is keyed on species and would have lost its Jirachi row.** `LogIndexOfSpecies` now folds story Shadow species back to the ordinary one (`WotBaseSpeciesForShadow`), so Shadow Jirachi fills the Jirachi row exactly as the flagged plain one used to -- **the roster array and its saved bit indices are untouched, so existing saves keep the bit they already have.** RULE: anything keyed on species must fold the Shadow species before lookup.
- **MEGAS ON THE TROPHY SCREENS.** New `WotGetDisplaySpecies(mon)` in `wot_mega_shop.c` returns the Mega form when the mon holds its own stone (matched against that species' `FORM_CHANGE_BATTLE_MEGA_EVOLUTION_ITEM` entry), else the plain species. Used by `hall_of_fame.c` (the champion screen -- note this is SAVED into the Hall of Fame record, so it persists in the HoF PC viewer) and `main_menu_bw.c`'s `LoadPartyMonIcons` (the continue/resume screen). Display only; battle is untouched, and eggs short-circuit.
- **THE FRONTIER FERRY: elevation mismatch, not a missing script.** The ship object sat at **elevation 3 on a water tile (elevation 1)** while the player talks to it from the dock at elevation 3. `ObjectEventDoesElevationMatch` treats **0 as a wildcard** on either side, and every vanilla SS Tidal uses 0 or 1 for exactly this reason -- ours was the odd one out, so A did nothing. Set to **0**. The script itself (`ShinTokyo_EventScript_FrontierFerry` -> `warp MAP_BATTLE_FRONTIER_OUTSIDE_WEST, 19, 67`) was always correct. **RULE: an object on water that is talked to from land needs elevation 0.**
- **SHADOW MONS AND MEGA EVOLUTION: no block exists, and none was added.** Audited every one of the ~20 `MON_DATA_IS_SHADOW` call sites, `CanMegaEvolve`, `CanActivateGimmick`, `AssignUsableGimmicks`, `GetBattleFormChangeTargetSpecies`, the player's trigger UI, and the `enum Gimmick` list (stock -- there is no WoT shadow gimmick occupying the slot). Nothing gates a Shadow Pokemon out of Mega Evolution. Also confirmed the substruct bitfield holding `isShadow`/`shadowOpened` sits at 64 of 96 bits, so no collision. **Prerequisites that WILL stop it and are the likely explanation: the Mega Ring must be in the bag (given in TradewindTown), the stone must be that species' own, and the species must actually have a Mega -- Shadow Jirachi and Shadow Lugia do not.**
- TEST CHECKLIST: catch the tower Jirachi and confirm it stays Shadow Jirachi (Steel/Dark, custom art) in the party, summary, post-game and continue screen, and that the Shadow Log's Jirachi row fills; a Shadow mon in the party shows aura + heart gauge; the ferry responds to A from the dock and lands at BattleFrontier_OutsideWest 19,67; a stone-carrying mon shows as its Mega in the Hall of Fame and on the continue screen; a Shadow mon holding its stone gets the Mega trigger with the Mega Ring in the bag.

Built with a fresh in-repo Python BPS encoder (SourceRead / SourceCopy / TargetRead) and checked by a **separate applier written from the spec** -- applied output byte-identical to the built ROM, all three embedded CRCs check. The encoder from the v1.1.0 round was not kept; **this one is worth keeping** (scratchpad `make_bps.py`) or porting into `Tools/`.

All three use the stock `Common_EventScript_FindItem`, which reads the item and the flag straight off the object, so they need no scripts of their own. Flags 0x4A5/0x4A6/0x4A7 (**next free: 0x4A8**).
- **The overworld sprites are the bag icons, pixel-for-pixel.** Every mega stone icon carries its art inside a 14x14 box, so `tools/build_mega_stone_ow.py` just crops that and centres it on a 16x16 canvas -- nothing scaled, no colour remapped. Because the indices are untouched, each object event points at the icon's OWN palette (`graphics/items/icon_palettes/<stone>.pal`) via a new palette tag. **The shared `OBJ_EVENT_PAL_TAG_GROUND_ITEMS` palette could NOT be reused -- all 16 of its slots are already spoken for by the Potion / Carved Mask / Catalpa Bow pickups, and the stones are blue-heavy.** No two stones share a map, so a palette each costs nothing at runtime. New gfx ids 420-422 (`NUM_OBJ_EVENT_GFX` 420 -> 423), palette tags 0x8006-0x8008.
- **THE MINISTER NOW TRADES MEGA STONES** at the Shin-Tokyo docks (68,37), 10,000 flat, for whatever is at the FRONT of the party. **It hangs off the END of every one of his four quest branches** (idle / waiting / settle+rewards / Ryuden lead) -- each used to finish `release; end` and now falls through to the trade, so his story dialogue always plays first and is never interrupted or replaced.

A species with no Mega simply has nothing to sell and he says so, so the sale can never complete for a Pokemon that cannot Mega Evolve. **90 species in the build have a stone**, so this really does put every one behind one NPC as intended.
- **Six species have TWO stones and would otherwise have been unobtainable** -- Charizard/Raichu/Mewtwo (X and Y) and the Z variants of Absol, Garchomp and Lucario. The special reports the count in `VAR_0x8006` and takes a selection index in `VAR_0x8004`, and the script shows a picker when there are two. **The trick that makes the picker work: `dynmultichoice` runs `StringExpandPlaceholders` over every row, so a row whose entire text is `{STR_VAR_2}` renders as whatever the special buffered.** Worth remembering for any dynamic list.
- Price is `.set MINISTER_MEGA_STONE_PRICE, 10000` at the top of ShinTokyo/scripts.inc. Money and bag space are both checked before anything is handed over.
- **Joe's porymap round included in this build** (his edits, not mine): new wild tables for **MunenTunnel** and **NationalPark_Act2**; reworked land encounters for **Ashlands, DistortionWorld, TradewindTown, WalnutWoods**; **Route_2_2** fishing; new object events on **CelebiIsland** and **ShinTokyo**; Brawly repositioned in DistortionWorld_4. Everything compiles.
- **map.json round-trips exactly through `json.dumps(indent=2) + newline`** -- verified before editing, so scripted edits to porymap files add only the intended lines instead of reformatting the whole file under Joe's uncommitted work.

### 2026-08-27 (round 12) — GAME CORNER prize tables replaced + NINE NEW TMs (BUILT green 10:42, untested)
- **Joe's prize list replaces the vanilla dolls/TM counters entirely.** Counter 1 (object 4, was the doll clerk) sells **15 Pokemon**; counter 2 (object 9, was the TM clerk) sells **13 TMs and evolution items**. Both are ordered cheapest first.
- **NINE OF THE REQUESTED TMs DID NOT EXIST** and had to be created: this fork's `FOREACH_TM` was still the vanilla Emerald 50. Added `TM51 Draco Meteor · TM52 Close Combat · TM53 Scale Shot · TM54 Play Rough · TM55 Swords Dance · TM56 Nasty Plot · TM57 Scorching Sands · TM58 Earth Power · TM59 Knock Off`.
- **No item ids moved and saves are unaffected** — `include/constants/items.h` already reserves **100 TM slots (582-681)** with only 50 in use, and HMs sit separately at 682-689. `ENUM_TM` aliases `ITEM_TM_<MOVE> = ITEM_TM<n>` positionally off `FOREACH_TM`, so the nine new entries simply consumed already-allocated ids 632-640. **Check this before ever adding a TM: appending to FOREACH_TM is free until slot 100.**
- **`src/data/items.h` already had placeholder `[ITEM_TM51]..[ITEM_TM100]` entries** (name "TM51", `sQuestionMarksDesc`). They must be FILLED IN, not added alongside — adding duplicates produced nine `-Werror=override-init` "initialized field overwritten" errors. Only the descriptions needed writing.
- **Teachable learnsets regenerate themselves.** `tms_hms.h` is in `TEACHABLE_DEPS` in the Makefile and `P_LEARNSET_HELPER_TEACHABLE` is TRUE, so `make` re-ran `tools/learnset_helpers/make_teachables.py` and every species that legitimately learns these moves picked them up: Draco Meteor 76 species, Close Combat 144, Scale Shot 83, Play Rough 179, Swords Dance 305, Nasty Plot 191, Scorching Sands 82, Earth Power 222, Knock Off 333. **Nothing had to be hand-listed.**
- **Both counters use `dynmultichoice` (scrolling), not `multichoice`.** A plain multichoice window is sized `count*2` tile rows, so anything past ~6 entries runs off the bottom of a 20-row screen — useless for 15- and 13-row tables. `dynmultichoice left, top, ignoreBPress, maxBeforeScroll, initialSelected, DYN_MULTICHOICE_CB_NONE, <string ptrs...>`; cancel returns `MULTI_B_PRESSED` (127). Prices are printed inside each row with `{CLEAR_TO}` + `{FONT_SMALL}` so the whole price list is visible while browsing.
- **Coins are deducted only AFTER the prize is handed over**, so a full bag or a full party+PC can never charge for nothing (`MON_CANT_GIVE` creates nothing, so that path just reports and returns). Each purchase loops back to the list instead of closing it.
- **Porygon is the only prize that arrives holding an item** (Dubious Disc), via its own `givemon SPECIES_PORYGON, ..., item=ITEM_DUBIOUS_DISC` branch.
- **Two judgment calls Joe should confirm:** (1) his list read "Scorching Sands / Earth Power 4,000" — **both** are stocked, at 4,000 each; (2) prize Pokemon are handed over at **level 5**, set by `.set GAME_CORNER_PRIZE_LEVEL, 5` at the top of the map's scripts.inc — one line to retune all fifteen.
- Prices as specified: Gimmighoul 999 · Charcadet/Honedge/Toxel 2,000 · Goomy 2,500 · Jangmo-o/Greavard/Duraludon 3,000 · Dreepy 4,000 · Frigibax/Gible/Axew 5,000 · Rotom 6,500 · Type: Null 8,000 · Porygon 9,999 || Play Rough 3,000 · Scale Shot/Knock Off/Leader's Crest 3,500 · Scorching Sands/Earth Power/Metal Alloy/Auspicious Armor/Malicious Armor 4,000 · Swords Dance/Nasty Plot 6,000 · Draco Meteor/Close Combat 8,000.
- TEST CHECKLIST: both counters scroll through their full lists with prices aligned; buying deducts the right number of coins and returns to the list; buying with too few coins is refused; a full bag refuses the item without charging; the new TMs appear correctly named in the TM pocket and teach to a legal target; Porygon arrives holding a Dubious Disc.

### 2026-08-27 (round 11) — BLANK TEXT BOXES EVERYWHERE: BG0 scroll leaked by the map-name popup (BUILT green 09:18, untested)
- **Joe's report:** entering `MauvilleCity_GameCorner` from Shin Tokyo blanks every text box, and the start menu stays blank after leaving. **Not a Game Corner bug at all** — it reproduces on ANY warp taken while the map-name popup is still on screen. That door just happens to be ~13 tiles from the station arrival on the same row (y=42), so you reach it inside the popup's display window nearly every time.
- **ROOT CAUSE:** with `OW_POPUP_GENERATION == GEN_5` the BW popup slides by writing `REG_BG0VOFS` **from an HBlank callback** (`HBlankCB_DoublePopupWindow`, menu.c), and the ONLY thing that puts that register back is `HideMapNamePopUpWindow()`'s `SetGpuReg_ForcedBlank(REG_OFFSET_BG0VOFS, 0)`. Warping destroys the popup task with the map load, so that cleanup never runs. **Nothing else in the field ever writes BG0VOFS** — `FieldUpdateBgTilemapScroll()` only touches BG1–BG3 — and **BG0 is the layer every text box and menu is drawn on**, so they all render scrolled off screen, permanently and across maps. The map itself looks fine because it lives on the other BGs, which is what makes this read as "text is broken" rather than "the screen is scrolled".
- **Fix 1 (the real one):** `FieldClearVBlankHBlankCallbacks()` in overworld.c now forces `REG_OFFSET_BG0VOFS` back to 0 after killing the HBlank callback. That function is the choke point for **every** exit from the field (16 call sites — warps, battles, menus), so no warp path can leak the offset again.
- **Fix 2 (belt and braces):** `HBlankCB_DoublePopupWindow()` now returns early if `gPopupTaskId` is out of range or its task is inactive. It was dereferencing `gTasks[gPopupTaskId].data[2]` unguarded, so if it ever outlives the popup task by even a frame it would scroll BG0 by whatever junk sat in the dead slot.
- **RULE worth keeping: BG0 is the text/menu layer in the field and its scroll register is not maintained by anything.** Any effect that scrolls BG0 owns restoring it, and must survive being killed mid-animation by a map change.
- TEST CHECKLIST: enter Shin Tokyo and walk straight into the Game Corner while the map name is still on screen — text boxes and the start menu must be normal. Same for the jail, hideout and Mewtwo doors. Confirm the popup itself still slides correctly when NOT interrupted.

- **All three starters now evolve on the same schedule: 2nd stage at 16, 3rd stage at 36**, so no starter choice is punished by a late line. Vanilla levels were wildly uneven — Gible 24/48, Axew 38/48, Frigibax 35/54; Frigibax in particular didn't reach Baxcalibur until 54, well past the end of the hack's curve.
- Six edits, one `EVOLUTION({EVO_LEVEL, n, ...})` line each: `SPECIES_GABITE` 24→16 and `SPECIES_GARCHOMP` 48→36 (`gen_4_families.h`); `SPECIES_FRAXURE` 38→16 and `SPECIES_HAXORUS` 48→36 (`gen_5_families.h`); `SPECIES_ARCTIBAX` 35→16 and `SPECIES_BAXCALIBUR` 54→36 (`gen_9_families.h`).
- **Note: this is species data, so it is global, not starter-only** — any wild or gift Gible/Axew/Frigibax anywhere in the game evolves on the new schedule too. Trainer parties are unaffected (they name exact species). Randomizer runs are unaffected for the same reason: the rolled starter is a different species with its own evolution data.
- TEST CHECKLIST: raise each starter past 16 and 36 and confirm both evolutions fire; confirm the Pokédex evolution page shows the new levels.

### 2026-08-27 (round 9) — RANDOMIZER DIDN'T WORK AT ALL: flags were wiped by new game (BUILT green 08:15, untested)
- **ROOT CAUSE (Joe: "both ON and OFF gave the standard game"): `NewGameInitData()` calls `InitEventData()`, which does `memset(gSaveBlock1Ptr->flags, 0, ...)`.** The settings screen runs BEFORE `CB2_NewGame`, so every flag it set was erased moments later — the randomizer could never turn on from a new game, whichever way it was set. **RULE: nothing written to flags or vars before `CB2_NewGame` survives. A new-game-time choice must be parked outside the save block.**
- **Fix — deferred commit.** `WotQueueRandomizerSettings()` parks the choice in a BSS bitfield (which a new game does not touch) and **`WotApplyQueuedRandomizerSettings()` is called from the very END of `NewGameInitData()`** — after `InitPlayerTrainerId()`, so the randomizer seed also reads the *fresh* id rather than the outgoing save's. The queue is consumed on use so a later new game can't inherit a stale choice. The menu still writes the flags directly as well, which is what the bedroom-PC path needs (no new game to survive there).
- **Description box was clipping its second line.** 20 screen tile-rows cannot hold three fully-bordered boxes plus 5 option rows at 16px: header frame (4) + list frame (12) + a bordered 2-line description (6) = 22. The description window sat at rows 16–19 with its frame drawn *over* rows 16 and 19, leaving one usable line. Now the description box draws **side edges only** — the list's own bottom border at row 15 is the separator, and a bottom edge would land off-screen at row 20 — giving the window all four rows (32px). Text y moved 1 → 0; **`FONT_NORMAL` advances 15px per line (`gFontsMaxWidthHeight[FONT_NORMAL] = {8, 15}`), so two lines need the full 32px.**
- **LEFT/RIGHT no longer wrap** (Joe: "hard to tell which is on or off since we can scroll left and it wraps around"). LEFT always selects the left-hand value (OFF), RIGHT always the right-hand one (ON/RANDOM); pressing into the value you already have is a no-op — no sound, no redraw. A still toggles. Up/down still wrap, which is standard.
- **Inactive rows no longer look selected.** They used to draw OFF in the red "selected" colour even while greyed; now a row that isn't live draws *both* values plain, so red always and only means "this is what's in force". `DrawChoices()` also clears and repaints the whole window (labels included) rather than overdrawing in place.
- **Corrected a false description:** the START row said the settings "are permanent" — untrue, since the bedroom PC can change them until the starter is taken. Now says so.
- TEST CHECKLIST: **New Game → RANDOMIZER ON → the game is actually randomized** (this is the fix); OFF gives a stock game. Both description lines fully visible on every row. LEFT on an already-OFF row does nothing; RIGHT on an already-ON row does nothing. Sub-rows show both values plain while the master is OFF.

### 2026-08-26 (round 8) — RANDOMIZER SETTINGS SCREEN + randomized starters (BUILT green 23:57, untested)
- **The randomizer is now a real options page, not a yes/no prompt.** New file `src/wot_randomizer_menu.c` (+ `CB2_WotRandomizerMenu` / `WotStartRandomizerMenu(MainCallback)` in the header) — a five-row settings screen modelled beat-for-beat on `src/option_menu.c`: title bar, list of toggles with values right-aligned, WIN0 highlight bar over the selected row, and a description box that explains whatever is highlighted. Rows: **RANDOMIZER (OFF/ON) · STARTER PKMN · WILD PKMN · TRAINER PKMN (each OFF/RANDOM) · START**.
- **Per-category flags** — 0x4A2 `FLAG_WOT_RAND_STARTERS` · 0x4A3 `FLAG_WOT_RAND_WILD` · 0x4A4 `FLAG_WOT_RAND_TRAINERS` (next free **0x4A5+**), all gated behind the master 0x4A0. Nothing new in the save block; the three sub-toggles are drawn greyed and refuse input while the master is off, and a master-on-with-everything-off state folds back to off on commit. Flipping the master on for the first time turns all three sub-options on with it.
- **Starters are randomized INCLUDING their mugshots.** `WotRandomizeStarter()` + special `WotSetStarterSpecies` (slot in `VAR_0x8004` → rolled species out in `VAR_0x8005`). The Munen_village_2 choice scripts now `showmonpic VAR_0x8005, 10, 3` — **`showmonpic` and `givemon` both read their species through `VarGet`, so both accept a variable** — and branch on `FLAG_WOT_RAND_STARTERS` between the authored Gible/Frigibax/Axew (with their scripted movesets) and `givemon VAR_0x8005, 5` with a natural level-5 moveset. The portrait you see IS the mon you get.
- **Two ways in.** (1) **New Game:** `main_menu_bw.c` — the LIVE menu — routes the naming screen's callback through the settings page before `CB2_NewGame`, so it appears exactly once, right after the player enters their name. (2) **The bedroom PC:** `LittlerootTown_BrendansHouse_2F_EventScript_PC` (Munen's house warps here) offers RANDOMIZER SETTINGS on interact until `FLAG_RECEIVED_STARTER` is set, then reverts to a normal PC — so the choice is revisitable right up until the starter is taken, without a new file. Uses `fadescreen FADE_TO_BLACK` → `special WotOpenRandomizerMenu` → `waitstate`, returning via `CB2_ReturnToFieldContinueScriptPlayMapMusic`.
- **Two gotchas worth keeping.** `DrawOptionMenuChoice` is **static** in option_menu.c — copied locally as `DrawChoiceText`; it recolours a selected value by **overwriting bytes 2 and 5 of the string**, which is why every drawable value string must carry a `{COLOR GREEN}{SHADOW LIGHT_GREEN}` prefix (a bare `_("OFF")` gets its first glyphs corrupted). And a fade-out task must kick `BeginNormalPaletteFade` **once** behind a latch — calling it every frame restarts the fade forever and the screen never leaves.
- Old `Munen_village_2_Text_RandomizerAsk/Confirm/On` deleted (the screen replaces that prompt entirely).
- TEST CHECKLIST: New Game → name entry → settings page appears; arrows move the bar, LEFT/RIGHT/A toggles, sub-rows are dead while the master is OFF; START and B both commit and enter the game. Bedroom PC before the starter → same page, returns to the field. With STARTER PKMN on, all three Munen portraits show rolled species and the mon received matches the portrait; with it off, Gible/Frigibax/Axew with their authored moves.

### 2026-08-26 (round 7) — randomizer exemptions + real Yacht Key icon (BUILT green 23:14, untested)
- **SHADOW MONS ARE NOW EXEMPT FROM THE RANDOMIZER — this was a correctness bug, not flavour.** `struct TrainerMon` already carries `isShadow` ("Shadow: Yes" in trainers.party), so the exemption is AUTOMATIC and needs no hardcoded trainer list: every Shadow on every trainer, present and future, keeps its species. Without it the **Shadow Log would have been uncompletable** (it tracks by species) and all 46 custom Shadow battle sprites would have gone unused in randomizer runs.
- **Boss exemptions kept deliberately tiny** (`WotTrainerIsRandomizerExempt`, identified via TRAINER_BATTLE_PARAM since `struct Trainer` carries no id): only **TRAINER_WALLY_VR_5 (THE ONI)** and **TRAINER_WALLY_VR_4 (Draco's Armored Mewtwo)**. Rationale written into the source: randomizer players EXPECT bosses to be rolled, so this is not difficulty protection -- these two are the cases where the species is load-bearing OUTSIDE the battle. Draco's especially: the Mewtwo is handed over afterwards by `givemon`, which the randomizer never touches, so rolling that fight would mean fighting one thing and receiving another.
- Companion fix: the "skip scripted movesets" rule now keys off **whether the mon was actually swapped** (`monSpecies == partyData[monIndex].species`) rather than on randomizer mode globally -- so exempt Shadows and bosses keep their authored movesets even in a randomizer run.
- **Yacht Key has its own icon.** Was borrowing the Snag Machine's art. Now `graphics/items/icons/yacht_key.png` + `icon_palettes/yacht_key.pal`: the vanilla key sprite shape recoloured to brass-and-navy so it never reads as the Basement Key. Registered in all three required places -- `src/data/graphics/items.h`, **`include/graphics.h` externs (item icons reach item.c ONLY via these)**, and the item entry.
- TEST CHECKLIST: in a randomizer run, Shadow trainers still field the written Shadows (and the Log still fills); THE ONI and Draco's Mewtwo battles are unrandomized; ordinary trainers/wilds still roll; Yacht Key shows a brass key in the KEY ITEMS pocket.

- **YACHT (fisherman payoff).** New key item **ITEM_YACHT_KEY 919** replaces the TM Earthquake on BOTH fisherman reward paths (traded-Gyarados and KO'd-Gyarados). The Route 2 sign already asked "Hijack the yacht?" and answered "Failed!" -- it now checks the key first and boards. **New map `YachtInterior`** (12x10, LAYOUT_YACHT_INTERIOR, General+Lab tilesets) as a decorate-me placeholder: layout binaries generated, registered in layouts.json + map_groups.json, and **`.include`d in data/event_scripts.s** (forgetting that include is an undefined-reference link error, not a build error). Inside: three balls -- **Treecko / Torchic / Mudkip at Lv5, take exactly ONE** (FLAG_WOT_YACHT_STARTER_TAKEN hides all three; full-party/box handled). Both directions are SCRIPT warps on purpose: a real warp_event on the beach tile would teleport anyone who walked past the sign. Exit returns to Route 2 (34,28), the tile the player stands on to read the sign.
- **RANDOMIZER MODE** -- new `src/wot_randomizer.c` + `include/wot_randomizer.h`. Asked ONCE as the first beat of the Munen opening (double-confirmed, `FLAG_WOT_RANDOMIZER 0x4A0`), before any encounter can exist.
  - `WotRandomizeSpecies()` is a **pure deterministic function of (species, save seed)** -- avalanche hash seeded from `playerTrainerId`, so the same species always becomes the same replacement (coherent world), different per playthrough, and **nothing extra is written to the save**.
  - The roll happens in **NATIONAL DEX space** (`1..NATIONAL_DEX_COUNT` -> `NationalPokedexNumToSpecies`), which guarantees **gen 1-9 base forms only** -- no megas, regional forms, Shadow duplicates or out-of-dex entries. Holes from `P_FAMILY_*` switches are walked past (64 tries, validated with `gSpeciesInfo[x].baseHP != 0`).
  - **Two hook points only:** `CreateWildMon()` (the single funnel for grass/water/fishing/outbreaks) and the `CreateNPCTrainerPartyFromTrainer()` mon loop, where a single `monSpecies` local now feeds gender, ability lookup AND `CreateMon`. **LEVELS ARE NEVER TOUCHED** anywhere.
  - Scripted movesets are SKIPPED when randomizing (they belong to the original species); `CreateMon`'s level-appropriate moveset stands instead.
  - **Deliberately unaffected** (all bypass both hooks): `givemon` gifts, eggs, and `setwildbattle` statics -- so the starters, every Shadow legendary and all story Pokemon stay exactly as written. Verified `ScrCmd_setwildbattle` does not route through CreateWildMon.
  - Hash validated offline: deterministic, always inside 1..1025, 326 distinct outputs over 399 inputs, 1 identity collision, seed-sensitive.
- **BATTLE FRONTIER FERRY.** `OBJ_EVENT_GFX_SS_TIDAL` object at **ShinTokyo (13,47)**, hidden by `FLAG_WOT_HIDE_FRONTIER_FERRY 0x4A1` and revealed in the post-game ON_TRANSITION branch (with the explicit pre-clear `setflag`, per the round-6 hide-flag rule). Sails to **BattleFrontier_OutsideWest (19,67)** -- the exact harbour tile vanilla's SS Tidal uses from Lilycove/Slateport, so it lands where the base game expects.
- Flags consumed: 0x49F (yacht starter), 0x4A0 (randomizer), 0x4A1 (ferry). Next free 0x4A2+.
- TEST CHECKLIST: fisherman gives the key on both paths; sign boards the yacht; one starter only; disembark returns beside the sign; New Game randomizer prompt (and declining leaves the game vanilla); randomized wilds/trainers keep their levels; story mons unaffected; ferry appears only post-clear and lands at the frontier.
